Every ratio is baseline ÷ measured, so higher is always better: 2.00× means twice as fast, or half the area, or half the memory. Geomean covers LEC-proven, hand-written Pyrope only. norm marks a netlist whose behavioural registers yosys mapped, so it could be counted and timed exactly like the others. STA gate keeps a complete synthesis measurement visible when LiveHD OpenTimer disagrees with OpenSTA; it is excluded from the geomean and detailed in STA accuracy below.

STA accuracy — LiveHD OpenTimer vs OpenSTA

A diagnostic on the tables above, not a result in itself: this is how a delay that cannot be trusted gets found. Same netlist, same Liberty, same SDC — only the timer differs. Median error -2.62% over 602 paired timings; 102 more than 10.0% optimistic (LiveHD reporting a shorter path than OpenSTA, which is the dangerous direction — a design signed off on it would miss timing). The gate fails a test past ±10.0%.

Ratios are baseline ÷ measured, so higher is always better — 2.00× means twice as fast. All three simulators fold the same checksum or the test fails.

Equivalence — Pyrope vs Verilog

Both backends prove the same obligation, so the times compare and the verdicts check each other. lgcheck (yosys): 96 proven, 30 inconclusive, 18 timeout, 15 refuted, 13 skipped · lhd (cvc5): 157 proven, 13 skipped, 2 refuted. lhd is 5.13× the speed of lgcheck over the 96 test(s) where both reached the same verdict. 15 backend disagreement(s) — one engine proved what the other refuted, which is a code-generation or encoding bug rather than a design result.

A verdict is not boolean: proven and refuted are answers; timeout, inconclusive, unsupported and error are the absence of one. Until a test is proven, its QoR numbers above are reported but never aggregated — a Pyrope win might be a different circuit.

Equivalence — sources vs synthesized netlist

Two obligations over the same flat, mapped standard-cell design: LiveHD checks Pyrope vs netlist; both the Yosys-backed engine and LiveHD/cvc5 check Verilog vs netlist. Pyrope/cvc5: 294 proven, 18 unsupported, 12 refuted, 10 timeout. Verilog/Yosys: 168 proven, 138 inconclusive, 26 timeout, 2 refuted. Verilog/cvc5: 310 proven, 15 timeout, 5 refuted, 4 unsupported. 10 skipped. On the 170 row(s) where both Verilog-vs-netlist engines reached the same verdict, cvc5 is 6.04× the speed of the Yosys-backed one. 12 netlist row(s) refuted — synthesis or one source description changed the circuit. 2 checker disagreement(s) — lgyosys found a counterexample but cvc5 proved the identical Verilog-vs-netlist obligation; this is not counted as a confirmed synthesis failure. A timeout exhausted the budget; an inconclusive result returned earlier without either a proof or a counterexample. The table keeps all three checks explicit.
